    "originalFile": "\\documentclass[11pt]{article}\n\n\\usepackage[review]{acl}\n\n\\usepackage{times}\n\\usepackage{latexsym}\n\\usepackage[T1]{fontenc}\n\\usepackage[utf8]{inputenc}\n\\usepackage{microtype}\n\\usepackage{graphicx}\n\\usepackage{booktabs}\n\\usepackage{amsmath}\n\\usepackage{amssymb}\n\\usepackage{multirow}\n\\usepackage{xcolor}\n\n\\title{Per-Example Predictive Multiplicity in Hybrid Linear Recurrent Architectures}\n\n\\author{Anonymous}\n\n\\begin{document}\n\\maketitle\n\n\\begin{abstract}\nAdding attention to linear recurrent architectures is associated with per-example changes on almost entirely different examples across three controlled hybrid linear recurrent designs: pairwise overlap is at most 10\\%. Using the model zoo of \\citet{wang2025hybrid} (three architectures, five attention ratios, two scales), we decompose per-example effects and find that this predictive multiplicity \\citep{marx2020predictive} depends on task type ($\\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) rather than attention dose. Aggregate trends do not reach statistical significance ($n = 5$; Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .170$), yet per-example overlap saturates at 4\\% attention and remains flat across all four benchmarks. Naive majority vote fails to exploit this complementarity, suggesting that configurations interchangeable by aggregate metrics carry hidden consequences for per-example reliability.\n\\end{abstract}\n\n\\input{sections/introduction}\n\n\\input{sections/related_work}\n\n\\input{sections/method}\n\n\\input{sections/experiments}\n\n\\input{sections/conclusion}\n\n\\section*{Limitations}\n\nOur analysis has several limitations.\n\n\\paragraph{Single training runs.} Each configuration corresponds to a single training run; we cannot fully disentangle architectural from seed-specific effects, though the $3\\times$ within- vs.\\ cross-architecture Jaccard gap (\\S\\ref{ssec:decoupling}) provides proxy evidence. All per-example claims are conditioned on this limitation. With $n = 5$ attention ratios, the minimum achievable $p$-value is 0.017, contributing to LAMBADA's Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .170$ not reaching conventional significance. As a partial calibration, we compare within-seed Jaccard from Pythia 70M PolyPythias \\citep{vanderwal2025polypythias} against our cross-architecture values (Appendix~\\ref{app:seed_calibration}); within-seed overlap is 2.7--3.4$\\times$ higher, consistent with, but not definitive evidence for, architecture-driven divergence. Future work should conduct multi-seed training on matched architectures to fully isolate architectural from seed-specific effects.\n\n\\paragraph{Coverage and generalization.} We examine three architectures from one model zoo on four main benchmarks (two supplementary). The controlled design isolates architectural effects but limits generalization to other hybrid families. We compare only two scale points (340M, 1.3B). Our analysis does not cover Mamba, RWKV, or generation tasks; generalization to these architectures and task types remains open.\n\n\\paragraph{Pre-training only.} All models are evaluated without fine-tuning; per-example effects may not persist after task-specific adaptation. We evaluate only discrete accuracy; continuous metrics may reveal different multiplicity patterns.\n\n\\section*{Ethics Statement}\n\nThis work analyzes publicly available pretrained language models from the model zoo of \\citet{wang2025hybrid}. No new data collection, human subjects research, or model training is involved. We identify per-example behavioral differences across architectures, which may inform practitioners about the limitations of aggregate benchmark comparisons but does not introduce new risks.\n\n\\section*{Code and Data Availability}\n\nAll models are from the publicly available model zoo of \\citet{wang2025hybrid}. Our analysis code and per-example evaluation outputs will be released upon publication.\n\n\\bibliography{references}\n\n\\appendix\n\\input{sections/appendix}\n\n\\end{document}\n",

_source line 5312 · 2026-05-22T17:12:23.802Z_

[Director 指令] ## [Patch] exp-005 4 架构 Jaccard 扩展——整合到 Appendix

**新实验结果已就绪。** 在原 3 架构（GDN, RetNet, HGRN2）基础上加入 GLA，形成 4 架构 6 对 pairwise Jaccard。所有结果一致：Jaccard ≤ 0.13，permutation test p < 0.001。

### 数据

**ARC-Easy (n=2376, 340M h3-1):**
| Pair | J_helped | J_hurt |
|------|---------|--------|
| GDN-RetNet | 0.089 | 0.114 |
| GDN-HGRN2 | 0.122 | 0.105 |
| GDN-GLA | 0.114 | 0.106 |
| RetNet-HGRN2 | 0.087 | 0.115 |
| RetNet-GLA | 0.112 | 0.110 |
| HGRN2-GLA | 0.098 | 0.087 |
| Mean | 0.103 | 0.106 |

**LAMBADA (n=5153, 340M h3-1):**
| Pair | J_helped | J_hurt |
|------|---------|--------|
| GDN-RetNet | 0.128 | 0.121 |
| GDN-HGRN2 | 0.123 | 0.083 |
| GDN-GLA | 0.124 | 0.100 |
| RetNet-HGRN2 | 0.108 | 0.083 |
| RetNet-GLA | 0.123 | 0.107 |
| HGRN2-GLA | 0.127 | 0.086 |
| Mean | 0.122 | 0.097 |

所有 24 个 permutation test p < 0.001 (z = 3.78–12.04)。

### 整合方案

**1. 新 Appendix section "Extended Architecture Validation"**
- 说明：从 m-a-p zoo 扩展到第 4 个架构 GLA (Gated Linear Attention)
- 表格：6 对 Jaccard（格式同 Table 2）
- 解读：GLA 与原 3 架构的 pairwise Jaccard 无显著差异，pattern 在 4 架构上 robust

**2. §4.1 或 §4.3 加一句引用**
例如："An extended analysis including a fourth architecture (GLA) confirms that cross-architecture Jaccard remains uniformly ≤ 0.13 across all six pairs (Appendix X)."

**3. Limitations 更新**
当前写 "three architectures"——更新为 "three primary architectures (with a fourth, GLA, confirming the pattern in Appendix X)"

**4. Abstract 可选更新**
如果不影响页数，可改 "three" → "three (extended to four)" 或保持简洁

### 约束
- 不要改变主文 Table 2 的现有数据（那是 1.3B 的结果，GLA 只有 340M）
- GLA 数据放 Appendix，明确标注 "340M, h3-1 configuration"
- 引用验证不需要（无新引用）

730	
731	Seeds thus produce comparable or higher amounts of per-example disagreement ($\alpha$) but on more overlapping examples (higher $J$). Cross-architecture variation produces per-example effects on less overlapping examples (lower $J$), consistent with architecture-driven divergence producing qualitatively different per-example patterns than seed noise. The within-seed Jaccard is 2.7--4.3$\times$ higher than cross-architecture Jaccard across both benchmarks.
732	
733	Three caveats qualify this comparison. First, scale mismatch: Pythia 70M is $5\times$ smaller than our smallest models (340M); attempts to use Pythia 410M failed repeatedly. Second, architecture family: Pythia is a standard Transformer, not a hybrid recurrent architecture, so this is a cross-family reference point rather than a matched control. Third, accuracy regime: Pythia 70M accuracy (LAMBADA 0.17--0.23, ARC-Easy ${\sim}$0.38) is far below our models (${\sim}$0.50, ${\sim}$0.72), and Jaccard is affected by base rates; the 2.7--4.3$\times$ ratio may differ at accuracy regimes closer to our main experiments. These limitations restrict the comparison to a reference point for seed-variation magnitude rather than definitive evidence for architecture-driven effects.
734

79	
80	\subsection{Robustness}\label{ssec:robustness}
81	
82	Removing GDN weakens LAMBADA convergence from $\rho = -1.0$ to $\rho = -0.7$ ($p = .233$) and reverses MMLU convergence from $\rho = -0.7$ to $\rho = +0.6$, indicating that the MMLU trend was entirely driven by GDN's 16-head configuration. GDN's h3-1 uses 16 attention heads rather than 32 in all other configurations (Appendix~\ref{app:gdn_confound}). The 16-vs-32 head asymmetry could contribute to GDN's faster convergence by limiting attention capacity, though disentangling this from architectural differences would require matched-head experiments not available in the zoo. The RetNet--HGRN2 pair preserves the decoupling: all hybrid $\sigma \leq 0.008$ while Jaccard saturates at 0.086--0.108. The aggregate convergence result is therefore dependent on GDN inclusion; the decoupling result is not. Full sensitivity analyses appear in Appendix~\ref{app:sensitivity}.
83	
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01T1rjmHfde7a1QsWrMgGLLi`</summary>

```text
33	Several benchmarks show positive or near-zero $\rho$ (BoolQ: $+0.2$; ARC-C: $+0.6$; HellaSwag: $+0.4$; WinoGrande: $+0.1$; SciQ: $+0.1$), indicating that attention does not systematically reduce cross-architecture variance on reasoning or comprehension tasks. The contrast with LAMBADA is consistent with attention's convergence benefit being specific to tasks requiring direct token-level recall \citep{arora2024zoology,arora2024based} rather than multi-step inference.
34	
35	\paragraph{Regression to the mean.} The direction of LAMBADA's aggregate convergence is consistent with regression to the mean: GDN (highest pure accuracy, 0.543) declines to 0.513 under h3-1, while HGRN2 (lowest, 0.484) rises to 0.514, yielding a $5\times$ $\sigma$ compression ($0.024 \to 0.005$). However, three observations argue against a purely statistical artifact. First, the $5\times$ reduction in cross-architecture $\sigma$ far exceeds typical regression amplitudes. Second, the direction is entirely determined by architecture identity, not random fluctuation: HGRN2 accuracy increases monotonically across all attention ratios, while GDN decreases monotonically---a pattern inconsistent with noise-driven mean reversion. Third, per-example analysis (\S\ref{ssec:per_example}) reveals architecture-specific patterns: GDN's net $-153$ examples and HGRN2's net $+153$ examples reflect distinct helped/hurt profiles with low cross-architecture Jaccard overlap, indicating that the aggregate convergence arises from structured, architecture-dependent per-example changes rather than symmetric regression.
36	
37	\section{Scale-Dependent Convergence}\label{app:scale_convergence}
```

</details>

          " ",
          "-Removing GDN weakens LAMBADA convergence from $\\rho = -1.0$ to $\\rho = -0.7$ ($p = .233$) and reverses MMLU convergence from $\\rho = -0.7$ to $\\rho = +0.6$, indicating that the MMLU trend was entirely driven by GDN's 16-head configuration. GDN's h3-1 uses 16 attention heads rather than 32 in all other configurations (Appendix~\\ref{app:gdn_confound}). The 16-vs-32 head asymmetry could contribute to GDN's faster convergence by limiting attention capacity, though disentangling this from architectural differences would require matched-head experiments not available in the zoo.           "+Removing GDN weakens LAMBADA convergence from $\\rho = -1.0$ to $\\rho = -0.7$ ($p = .233$) and reverses MMLU convergence from $\\rho = -0.7$ to $\\rho = +0.6$, indicating that the MMLU trend was entirely driven by GDN's 16-head configuration. The 16-vs-32 head asymmetry could contribute to GDN's faster convergence by limiting attention capacity, though disentangling this from architectural differences would require matched-head experiments not available in the zoo. An extended analysis including a fourth architecture (GLA) confirms that cross-architecture Jaccard remains uniformly $\\leq 0.13$ across all six pairs (Appendix~\\ref{app:four_arch}).     "originalFile": "\\documentclass[11pt]{article}\n\n\\usepackage[review]{acl}\n\n\\usepackage{times}\n\\usepackage{latexsym}\n\\usepackage[T1]{fontenc}\n\\usepackage[utf8]{inputenc}\n\\usepackage{microtype}\n\\usepackage{graphicx}\n\\usepackage{booktabs}\n\\usepackage{amsmath}\n\\usepackage{amssymb}\n\\usepackage{multirow}\n\\usepackage{xcolor}\n\n\\title{Per-Example Predictive Multiplicity in Hybrid Linear Recurrent Architectures}\n\n\\author{Anonymous}\n\n\\begin{document}\n\\maketitle\n\n\\begin{abstract}\nAdding attention to linear recurrent architectures is associated with per-example changes on almost entirely different examples across three controlled hybrid linear recurrent designs: pairwise overlap is at most 10\\%. Using the model zoo of \\citet{wang2025hybrid} (three architectures, five attention ratios, two scales), we decompose per-example effects and find that this predictive multiplicity \\citep{marx2020predictive} depends on task type ($\\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) rather than attention dose. Aggregate trends do not reach statistical significance ($n = 5$; Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .170$), yet per-example overlap saturates at 4\\% attention and remains flat across all four benchmarks. Naive majority vote fails to exploit this complementarity, suggesting that configurations interchangeable by aggregate metrics carry hidden consequences for per-example reliability.\n\\end{abstract}\n\n\\input{sections/introduction}\n\n\\input{sections/related_work}\n\n\\input{sections/method}\n\n\\input{sections/experiments}\n\n\\input{sections/conclusion}\n\n\\section*{Limitations}\n\nOur analysis has several limitations.\n\n\\paragraph{Single training runs.} Each configuration corresponds to a single training run; we cannot fully disentangle architectural from seed-specific effects, though the $3\\times$ within- vs.\\ cross-architecture Jaccard gap (\\S\\ref{ssec:decoupling}) provides proxy evidence. All per-example claims are conditioned on this limitation. With $n = 5$ attention ratios, the minimum achievable $p$-value is 0.017, contributing to LAMBADA's Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .170$ not reaching conventional significance. As a partial calibration, we compare within-seed Jaccard from Pythia 70M PolyPythias \\citep{vanderwal2025polypythias} against our cross-architecture values (Appendix~\\ref{app:seed_calibration}); within-seed overlap is 2.7--4.3$\\times$ higher, consistent with, but not definitive evidence for, architecture-driven divergence. Future work should conduct multi-seed training on matched architectures to fully isolate architectural from seed-specific effects.\n\n\\paragraph{Coverage and generalization.} We examine three architectures from one model zoo on four main benchmarks (two supplementary). The controlled design isolates architectural effects but limits generalization to other hybrid families. We compare only two scale points (340M, 1.3B). Our analysis does not cover Mamba, RWKV, or generation tasks; generalization to these architectures and task types remains open.\n\n\\paragraph{Pre-training only.} All models are evaluated without fine-tuning; per-example effects may not persist after task-specific adaptation. We evaluate only discrete accuracy; continuous metrics may reveal different multiplicity patterns.\n\n\\section*{Ethics Statement}\n\nThis work analyzes publicly available pretrained language models from the model zoo of \\citet{wang2025hybrid}. No new data collection, human subjects research, or model training is involved. We identify per-example behavioral differences across architectures, which may inform practitioners about the limitations of aggregate benchmark comparisons but does not introduce new risks.\n\n\\section*{Code and Data Availability}\n\nAll models are from the publicly available model zoo of \\citet{wang2025hybrid}. Our analysis code and per-example evaluation outputs will be released upon publication.\n\n\\bibliography{references}\n\n\\appendix\n\\input{sections/appendix}\n\n\\end{document}\n",

[Director 指令] ## [Patch] Appendix 升级：4-arch → 5-arch Jaccard

DeltaNet 评估完成，现在有 5 架构 10 对 pairwise Jaccard。升级 Appendix 表格和相关 claim。

### 数据（5-arch, 10 pairs）

**ARC-Easy (n=2376, 340M h3-1):**
| Pair | J_helped | J_hurt |
|------|---------|--------|
| GDN-RetNet | 0.089 | 0.114 |
| GDN-HGRN2 | 0.122 | 0.105 |
| GDN-GLA | 0.114 | 0.106 |
| GDN-DeltaNet | 0.108 | 0.098 |
| RetNet-HGRN2 | 0.087 | 0.115 |
| RetNet-GLA | 0.112 | 0.110 |
| RetNet-DeltaNet | 0.138 | 0.098 |
| HGRN2-GLA | 0.098 | 0.087 |
| HGRN2-DeltaNet | 0.089 | 0.103 |
| GLA-DeltaNet | 0.100 | 0.109 |
| **Mean** | **0.106** | **0.105** |

**LAMBADA (n=5153, 340M h3-1):**
| Pair | J_helped | J_hurt |
|------|---------|--------|
| GDN-RetNet | 0.128 | 0.121 |
| GDN-HGRN2 | 0.123 | 0.083 |
| GDN-GLA | 0.124 | 0.100 |
| GDN-DeltaNet | 0.117 | 0.087 |
| RetNet-HGRN2 | 0.108 | 0.083 |
| RetNet-GLA | 0.123 | 0.107 |
| RetNet-DeltaNet | 0.126 | 0.094 |
| HGRN2-GLA | 0.127 | 0.086 |
| HGRN2-DeltaNet | 0.108 | 0.086 |
| GLA-DeltaNet | 0.134 | 0.098 |
| **Mean** | **0.122** | **0.095** |

全部 40 个 permutation test p ≤ 0.002 (z = 3.49–12.04)。

### 修改项

1. **Appendix 表格**：从 6 对升级到 10 对。label 从 `four_arch` 改为 `extended_arch`。标题改为 "Extended Architecture Validation (Five Architectures)"

3. **Limitations**：
   - "a fourth architecture (GLA) confirms the pattern" → "two additional architectures (GLA and DeltaNet) confirm the pattern at 340M (Appendix X)"

4. **Abstract 不改**（主分析仍是 3 架构）

### 不要改的

          " ",
          " \\subsection{Robustness}\\label{ssec:robustness}",
          " ",
          "-Removing GDN weakens LAMBADA convergence from $\\rho = -1.0$ to $\\rho = -0.7$ ($p = .233$) and reverses MMLU convergence from $\\rho = -0.7$ to $\\rho = +0.6$, indicating that the MMLU trend was entirely driven by GDN's 16-head configuration. GDN's h3-1 uses 16 attention heads rather than 32 in all other configurations (Appendix~\\ref{app:gdn_confound}). The 16-vs-32 head asymmetry could contribute to GDN's faster convergence by limiting attention capacity, though disentangling this from architectural differences would require matched-head experiments not available in the zoo. The RetNet--HGRN2 pair preserves the decoupling: all hybrid $\\sigma \\leq 0.008$ while Jaccard saturates at 0.086--0.108. The aggregate convergence result is therefore dependent on GDN inclusion; the decoupling result is not. An extended analysis including a fourth architecture (GLA) confirms that cross-architecture Jaccard remains uniformly $\\leq 0.13$ across all six pairs (Appendix~\\ref{app:four_arch}). Full sensitivity analyses appear in Appendix~\\ref{app:sensitivity}.",
          "+Removing GDN weakens LAMBADA convergence from $\\rho = -1.0$ to $\\rho = -0.7$ ($p = .233$) and reverses MMLU convergence from $\\rho = -0.7$ to $\\rho = +0.6$, indicating that the MMLU trend was entirely driven by GDN's 16-head configuration. GDN's h3-1 uses 16 attention heads rather than 32 in all other configurations (Appendix~\\ref{app:gdn_confound}). The 16-vs-32 head asymmetry could contribute to GDN's faster convergence by limiting attention capacity, though disentangling this from architectural differences would require matched-head experiments not available in the zoo. The RetNet--HGRN2 pair preserves the decoupling: all hybrid $\\sigma \\leq 0.008$ while Jaccard saturates at 0.086--0.108. The aggregate convergence result is therefore dependent on GDN inclusion; the decoupling result is not. An extended analysis including a fourth architecture (GLA) confirms that cross-architecture Jaccard remains uniformly $\\leq 0.13$ across all six pairs (Appendix~\\ref{app:extended_arch}). Using the model zoo of \\citet{wang2025hybrid} (three architectures, five attention ratios, two scales), we decompose per-example effects and find that this predictive multiplicity \\citep{marx2020predictive} depends on task type ($\\alpha = 0.14$--$0.30$) rather than attention dose. Aggregate trends do not reach statistical significance ($n = 5$; Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .170$), yet per-example overlap saturates at 4\\% attention and remains flat across all four benchmarks. Naive majority vote fails to exploit this complementarity, suggesting that configurations interchangeable by aggregate metrics carry hidden consequences for per-example reliability.\n\\end{abstract}\n\n\\input{sections/introduction}\n\n\\input{sections/related_work}\n\n\\input{sections/method}\n\n\\input{sections/experiments}\n\n\\input{sections/conclusion}\n\n\\section*{Limitations}\n\nOur analysis has several limitations.\n\n\\paragraph{Single training runs.} Each configuration corresponds to a single training run; we cannot fully disentangle architectural from seed-specific effects, though the $3\\times$ within- vs.\\ cross-architecture Jaccard gap (\\S\\ref{ssec:decoupling}) provides proxy evidence. All per-example claims are conditioned on this limitation. With $n = 5$ attention ratios, the minimum achievable $p$-value is 0.017, contributing to LAMBADA's Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .170$ not reaching conventional significance. As a partial calibration, we compare within-seed Jaccard from Pythia 70M PolyPythias \\citep{vanderwal2025polypythias} against our cross-architecture values (Appendix~\\ref{app:seed_calibration}); within-seed overlap is 2.7--4.3$\\times$ higher, consistent with, but not definitive evidence for, architecture-driven divergence. Future work should conduct multi-seed training on matched architectures to fully isolate architectural from seed-specific effects.\n\n\\paragraph{Coverage and generalization.} We examine three primary architectures from one model zoo on four main benchmarks (two supplementary); a fourth architecture (GLA) confirms the pattern at 340M (Appendix~\\ref{app:four_arch}). The controlled design isolates architectural effects but limits generalization to other hybrid families. We compare only two scale points (340M, 1.3B). Our analysis does not cover Mamba, RWKV, or generation tasks; generalization to these architectures and task types remains open.\n\n\\paragraph{Pre-training only.} All models are evaluated without fine-tuning; per-example effects may not persist after task-specific adaptation. We evaluate only discrete accuracy; continuous metrics may reveal different multiplicity patterns.\n\n\\section*{Ethics Statement}\n\nThis work analyzes publicly available pretrained language models from the model zoo of \\citet{wang2025hybrid}. No new data collection, human subjects research, or model training is involved. We identify per-example behavioral differences across architectures, which may inform practitioners about the limitations of aggregate benchmark comparisons but does not introduce new risks.\n\n\\section*{Code and Data Availability}\n\nAll models are from the publicly available model zoo of \\citet{wang2025hybrid}. Our analysis code and per-example evaluation outputs will be released upon publication.\n\n\\bibliography{references}\n\n\\appendix\n\\input{sections/appendix}\n\n\\end{document}\n",

We identify per-example behavioral differences across architectures, which may inform practitioners about the limitations of aggregate benchmark comparisons but does not introduce new risks.\n\n\\section*{Code and Data Availability}\n\nAll models are from the publicly available model zoo of \\citet{wang2025hybrid}. Our analysis code and per-example evaluation outputs will be released upon publication.\n\n\\bibliography{references}\n\n\\appendix\n\\input{sections/appendix}\n\n\\end{document}\n",
    "structuredPatch": [
      {
        "oldStart": 41,
        "oldLines": 7,
        "newStart": 41,
        "newLines": 7,
        "lines": [
          " ",
          " \\paragraph{Single training runs.} Each configuration corresponds to a single training run; we cannot fully disentangle architectural from seed-specific effects, though the $3\\times$ within- vs.\\ cross-architecture Jaccard gap (\\S\\ref{ssec:decoupling}) provides proxy evidence. All per-example claims are conditioned on this limitation. With $n = 5$ attention ratios, the minimum achievable $p$-value is 0.017, contributing to LAMBADA's Bonferroni-adjusted $p = .170$ not reaching conventional significance. As a partial calibration, we compare within-seed Jaccard from Pythia 70M PolyPythias \\citep{vanderwal2025polypythias} against our cross-architecture values (Appendix~\\ref{app:seed_calibration}); within-seed overlap is 2.7--4.3$\\times$ higher, consistent with, but not definitive evidence for, architecture-driven divergence. Future work should conduct multi-seed training on matched architectures to fully isolate architectural from seed-specific effects.",
          " ",
          "-\\paragraph{Coverage and generalization.} We examine three primary architectures from one model zoo on four main benchmarks (two supplementary); a fourth architecture (GLA) confirms the pattern at 340M (Appendix~\\ref{app:extended_arch}). The controlled design isolates architectural effects but limits generalization to other hybrid families. We compare only two scale points (340M, 1.3B). Our analysis does not cover Mamba, RWKV, or generation tasks; generalization to these architectures and task types remains open.",
          "+\\paragraph{Coverage and generalization.} We examine three primary architectures from one model zoo on four main benchmarks (two supplementary); two additional architectures (GLA and DeltaNet) confirm the pattern at 340M (Appendix~\\ref{app:extended_arch}). The controlled design isolates architectural effects but limits generalization to other hybrid families. We compare only two scale points (340M, 1.3B). Our analysis does not cover Mamba, RWKV, or generation tasks; generalization to these architectures and task types remains open.",
          " ",
          " \\paragraph{Pre-training only.} All models are evaluated without fine-tuning; per-example effects may not persist after task-specific adaptation. We evaluate only discrete accuracy; continuous metrics may reveal different multiplicity patterns.",
          " "
        ]
      }
